- **Outputscale**: 2.544, which is very high. This means the overall variance of the function is significant, so the function might have abrupt changes or high variability.

The challenge is that f_min is still stuck, so we need to focus on exploration to find new areas that might have lower values. Possible acquisition functions to consider:
1. **EI (Expected Improvement)**: Good for exploitation but might not help if we're stuck. Since f_min hasn't changed, maybe EI isn't enough unless there's a region with high uncertainty where improvement is possible.
2. **LogEI**: Similar to EI but might focus on regions with higher uncertainty. Could be better if the variance is high.
3. **UCB**: Balances exploration and exploitation via the mean plus beta times std. Since the outputscale is high, the std might be significant, so UCB could push towards high variance areas.
4. **PosSTD**: Directly targets regions with high posterior standard deviation. Given the high outputscale, those regions might be promising. However, it was used at N=21 and N=22, but maybe it's okay to use again if needed.
5. **qMES or qPES**: These entropy-based methods focus on reducing uncertainty about the global minimum. Since we're still stuck, they might help find informative regions.
6. **TS (Thompson Sampling)**: Stochastic exploration, which was used at N=22 and N=23. It might be worth continuing if it helped break the clustering.
7. **qKG**: Knowledge gradient, which looks ahead to maximize expected improvement. Might be good for remaining steps.

Possible acquisition functions to consider:
1. **EI (Expected Improvement)**: Good for exploitation but might not help if we're stuck. Since f_min hasn't changed, maybe EI isn't enough unless there's a region with high uncertainty where improvement is possible.
2. **LogEI**: Similar to EI but might focus on regions with higher uncertainty. Could be better if the variance is high.
3. **UCB**: Balances exploration and exploitation via the mean plus beta times std. Since the outputscale is high, the std might be significant, so UCB could push towards high variance areas.
4. **PosSTD**: Directly targets regions with high posterior standard deviation. Given the high outputscale, those regions might be promising. However, it was used at N=21 and N=22, but maybe it's okay to use again if needed.
5. **qMES or qPES**: These entropy-based methods focus on reducing uncertainty about the global minimum. Since we're still stuck, they might help find informative regions.
6. **TS (Thompson Sampling)**: Stochastic exploration, which was used at N=22 and N=23. It might be worth continuing if it helped break the clustering.
7. **qKG**: Knowledge gradient, which looks ahead to maximize expected improvement. Might be good for remaining steps.

2. **LogEI**: Similar to EI but might focus on regions with higher uncertainty. Could be better if the variance is high.
3. **UCB**: Balances exploration and exploitation via the mean plus beta times std. Since the outputscale is high, the std might be significant, so UCB could push towards high variance areas.
4. **PosSTD**: Directly targets regions with high posterior standard deviation. Given the high outputscale, those regions might be promising. However, it was used before, but maybe it's still needed.
5. **qMES or qPES**: These entropy-based methods focus on reducing uncertainty about the global minimum. Since we're still stuck, they might help find informative regions.
6. **TS (Thompson Sampling)**: Stochastic exploration, which was used recently (N=31 and N=32). It might be worth continuing if it helped break the clustering.
7. **qKG**: Knowledge gradient, which looks ahead to maximize expected improvement. Might be good for remaining steps.

- **N: 35** (35 points evaluated so far)
- **Remaining iterations: 20** (plenty left, so exploration is still an option)
- **D: 2** (two-dimensional search space, manageable)
- **f_range**: The objective function values range from -0.632 (the current best) up to 150.900. The mean is 19.954 with a high standard deviation of 33.959. This indicates a lot of variability in the function values, but the minimum hasn't improved since N=26 (since the last update, it's still -0.632). That's a problem because we've done 35 evaluations without finding a better minimum.
- **Shortest distance**: 0.1258, which is moderate. This suggests that the last point is somewhat close to another point, indicating possible over-exploitation in a local area but not as extreme as before.
- **Lengthscales**: The kernel's lengthscales are between 0.505 and 0.753, with a mean of 0.629 and standard deviation 0.124. These values are moderate, indicating the model thinks the function has some structure but isn't too rough or too smooth.
- **Outputscale**: 6.7234, which is very high. This means the overall variance of the function is significant, so the function might have abrupt changes or high variability.
The key points here are that the minimum hasn't improved in several iterations (since N=26), and the outputscale is extremely high, indicating high variance. The lengthscales are moderate, so the function isn't extremely rough but still has some structure. The shortest distance is moderate (0.1258), suggesting some local exploration but not extreme clustering.
The user wants an acquisition function that can help find a better minimum. The previous choices have included UCB, PosSTD, TS, and others. The challenge is that f_min is still stuck, so we need to focus on exploration to find new areas that might have lower values. However, since there are still 20 iterations left, we can afford some exploration.
Possible acquisition functions to consider:
1. **EI (Expected Improvement)**: Good for exploitation but might not help if we're stuck. Since f_min hasn't changed, maybe EI isn't enough unless there's a region with high uncertainty where improvement is possible.
2. **LogEI**: Similar to EI but might focus on regions with higher uncertainty. Could be better if the variance is high.
3. **UCB**: Balances exploration and exploitation via the mean plus beta times std. Since the outputscale is high, the std might be significant, so UCB could push towards high variance areas.
4. **PosSTD**: Directly targets regions with high posterior standard deviation. Given the high outputscale, those regions might be promising. However, it was used before, but maybe it's still needed.
5. **qMES or qPES**: These entropy-based methods focus on reducing uncertainty about the global minimum. Since we're still stuck, they might help find informative regions.
6. **TS (Thompson Sampling)**: Stochastic exploration, which was used recently (N=31 and N=32). It might be worth continuing if it helped break the clustering.
7. **qKG**: Knowledge gradient, which looks ahead to maximize expected improvement. Might be good for remaining steps.
Looking at the previous steps, the last few choices were PosSTD (N=28), TS (N=29), PosSTD (N=30), TS (N=31), UCB (N=32), UCB (N=33), and UCB again (N=34). The problem is that f_min is still the same.
